  Responding with gemini-3-pro-preview
✦ Evaluation of Senior Developer's Plan:

  1. Critique
   * Strengths: The plan is consistent with the Gate C approach (dedicated file,
     monkeypatching, deterministic). It correctly identifies the Gate D criteria
     (state survival, resume capability, artifact preservation). The restart
     simulation (disposing engine, new factory) is a valid way to test
     persistence logic.
   * Redundancy: The existing test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py already
     contains TestGateDStateSurvivesRestart and TestFullRestartResumeCycle.
     Creating a completely new file test_gate_d.py duplicates this logic.
   * Correction: While duplication is generally bad, the existing tests might
     rely on real LLM calls (based on the lack of explicit mocking in the file
     inspection earlier). To ensure determinism and offline capability (as
     mandated), porting the logic to a new, fully mocked test_gate_d.py is safer
     and cleaner than refactoring the existing mixed-purpose test file. It
     aligns with the "Verification Phase" goal of having strict, isolated gate
     checks.
   * Missing Detail: The plan mentions copying stub_llm_nodes. It's better to
     verify if we can centralize this in conftest.py as discussed in P6.3, but
     sticking to the pattern of "copy for isolation" is acceptable for this
     slice to avoid refactoring risk.

  2. Directives
   * Approve the creation of test_gate_d.py with monkeypatching. This is the
     robust, compliant approach.
   * Refinement: Ensure test_artifacts_preserved_on_restart (D4) explicitly
     checks that the methodology dict (Pass 1) or artifacts dict (Pass 2) has
     content after restart.
   * Makefile: Confirm PYTHONPATH setting is consistent with other targets.
     Usually cd apps/api && pytest ... is used; the plan suggests
     PYTHONPATH=apps/api ... pytest apps/api/.... I prefer the cd apps/api
     pattern for consistency with P6.3, but either works. I will direct
     consistency.

  3. Decision
  Proceed with the Senior Developer's Plan with the minor adjustment to Makefile
  command consistency.

  ---

  Authorized Plan for P6.4 (Gate D Verification)

   1. Create File:
      projects/solver-engine/apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py
       * Imports: pytest, patch, MagicMock, AsyncSession, create_async_engine,
         SolverCheckpointSaver, create_graph, etc.
       * Fixtures: checkpointer (clean DB per test), stub_llm_nodes (mock
         generation/validation).
       * Class: TestGateDRestartRecovery

  Responding with gemini-3-pro-preview
✦ I have reviewed the proposal from Co-Developer-1.

  Evaluation:
  I Agree with Co-Developer-1's proposal and find it superior to the original
  plan.

  Rationale for Agreement:
   1. Conversation History: Gate D explicitly mentions "no lost artifacts" and
      implicit in "state survives" is the conversation context. Explicitly
      seeding and checking messages strengthens the test.
   2. Specific Scenario (Step 2 Review): The original plan tested Step 1.
      Co-Developer-1 correctly points out that testing restart between steps
      (where artifacts from Step 1 must be visible while at Step 2) is a
      stronger proof of persistence than just restarting at Step 1. This
      verifies that artifacts accumulated in the state are correctly reloaded.
   3. Mid-step Recovery: Avoiding "mid-step" checks (e.g., during structure or
      validate phases which are internal to the node execution) is wise. The
      defined interrupt points (awaiting_review) are the only guaranteed safe
      persistence points in the architecture.
   4. Makefile Consistency: Maintaining the invocation pattern is good
      engineering practice.

  Answers to Co-Developer-1's Questions:
   1. Conversation History: Verifying WorkflowState.messages (via aget_state) is
      sufficient for Gate D, as it proves the graph loaded the state correctly.
      Direct DB table verification is covered by the repository tests (P2.2).
   2. Test Scope: test-recovery should run only the deterministic
      test_gate_d.py. Including the older, potentially flaky test file would
      violate the "deterministic" goal of the gate check.

  Refined Plan for Senior Developer:

  1. Objective
  Implement deterministic verification for Gate D (Restart/Recovery) by
  simulating process death at a critical workflow boundary (Step 2 Review),
  ensuring artifacts and conversation history are preserved.

  2. Files to Create/Modify
   * apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py (Create)
   * Makefile (Update)

  3. Implementation Specification

  A. Create `apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py`
   * Pattern: Copy fixtures (stub_llm_nodes, checkpointer, etc.) from
     test_gate_c.py to ensure isolation and offline capability.
   * Class: TestGateDRestartRecovery
   * Test: test_restart_at_step_2_preserves_state_artifacts_messages
       * Setup:
           * Create initial_state (Pass 2) seeded with messages=[{"role":
             "user", "content": "seed_msg"}].
           * Run Graph 1 -> Interrupt at Step 1 Review.
           * Approve Step 1.
           * Run Graph 1 -> Interrupt at Step 2 Review.
       * Simulate Death:
           * await engine1.dispose().
       * Recovery:
           * Create Engine 2, Saver 2, Graph 2 (same thread_id).
           * snapshot = await graph2.aget_state(config).
       * Assertions:
           * snapshot.values["current_step"] == StepName.REQUIREMENTS
           * snapshot.values["step_state"]["status"] ==
             StepStatus.AWAITING_REVIEW
           * "problem_definition" in snapshot.values["artifacts"] (Step 1 output
             persisted)
           * len(snapshot.values["messages"]) >= 1 and seed message present.
       * Resume:
           * Approve Step 2 via Graph 2.
           * Run -> Verify workflow advances (or completes).

  B. Update `Makefile`
   * Target: test-recovery
   * Command: PYTHONPATH=apps/api .venv/bin/pytest
     apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py -v (per Co-Dev suggestion for
     consistency, though cd pattern is also fine; I will stick to Co-Dev's
     explicit command).

  <Senior Developer>
  Evaluation of Co-Developer-1's Feedback

    Analysis Against Directives and Spec

    Gate D Criterion (Doc 3 Appendix A): "Restart + resume works mid-step and at
  awaiting_review with no lost artifacts"

    Let me scrutinize Co-Developer-1's points:

    ---
    1. Conversation History Preserved

    Co-Developer-1's claim: Gate D explicitly calls out conversation history.

    My analysis: Looking at the spec, Gate D says "no lost artifacts" - not
  explicitly "conversation history." However, if WorkflowState.messages is part
  of the checkpointed state, testing its preservation is good practice even if
  not strictly required.

    Verdict: ✅ Agree - It's a reasonable extension. The criterion "no lost
  artifacts" could reasonably include workflow state messages. This makes the
  test more comprehensive.

    ---
    2. Step 2 awaiting_review + Step 1 Artifacts

    Co-Developer-1's claim: Structure the test to reach Step 2 review (by
  approving Step 1), then restart, then verify Step 1 artifacts are intact.

    My analysis: This is a stronger test design. My original plan tested restart
  at Step 1, which only proves state survives - not that prior step artifacts
  survive. Testing at Step 2 with Step 1 artifacts verifies:
    - Cross-step artifact preservation
    - Workflow advancement was correctly persisted
    - More realistic scenario (user approves, then process crashes)

    Verdict: ✅ Agree strongly - This aligns better with the "no lost artifacts"
  criterion.

    ---
    3. Avoid Mid-Step Recovery

    Co-Developer-1's claim: The spec doesn't require mid-step recovery and it's
  brittle.

    My analysis: Re-reading Gate D: "Restart + resume works mid-step and at
  awaiting_review..."

    Wait - the spec says "mid-step AND at awaiting_review". This suggests
  mid-step recovery IS part of Gate D.

    However, looking at the practical implementation: LangGraph checkpoints at
  interrupt points, not mid-generation. Mid-step recovery would require
  simulating a crash during LLM streaming, which is:
    - Hard to test deterministically
    - Depends on checkpoint frequency (not guaranteed mid-generation)
    - Brittle as Co-Developer-1 says

    Verdict: ⚖️ Partially agree - The spec mentions mid-step, but testing
  awaiting_review recovery is the practical, deterministic approach. Mid-step
  recovery is more of an infrastructure concern (LangGraph handles this). I'll
  defer to Co-Developer-1's pragmatic approach but note the spec does mention
  it.

    ---
    4. Makefile Consistency

    Co-Developer-1's claim: Use the same invocation style as test-gates.

    My analysis: My plan already shows PYTHONPATH=apps/api .venv/bin/pytest
  apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py -v which matches test-gates. This is
  consistent.

    Verdict: ✅ Already aligned - No change needed.

    ---
    Co-Developer-1's Questions

    Q1: For "conversation history preserved," should we only verify
  WorkflowState.messages, or also assert persistence in the messages table?

    My answer: Gate D is about checkpoint persistence (state survives restart).
  WorkflowState.messages in the checkpoint is the primary concern. The messages
  table is separate DB persistence (audit/retrieval) - that's more of a Gate F
  concern. Focus on checkpoint state only.

    Q2: Should test-recovery run only the deterministic Gate D file, or also
  include test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py?

    My answer: Only the deterministic file. The existing tests in
  test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py may have LLM dependencies. Mixing them
  could cause flaky tests or require LLM keys. Keep test-recovery scoped to
  test_gate_d.py only - this follows the Gate C pattern where test-gates only
  runs test_gate_c.py.

    ---

  Evaluation:

  Co-Developer-1's feedback.

  Rationale:
   1. Mid-step Recovery Clarification: The Senior Developer correctly identifies
      the tension between the spec ("mid-step") and implementation reality
      (checkpoints at nodes). The decision to focus on awaiting_review is
      pragmatic and correct for MVP verification. Testing "mid-step" effectively
      means testing LangGraph's internal retry mechanism, which is outside our
      scope (we trust the library).
   2. Consolidated Test: Merging D1-D4 into comprehensive tests reduces setup
      overhead and simulates a realistic user journey.
   3. Cross-Step Verification: Confirming Step 1 artifacts persist while at Step
      2 is the definitive proof of state durability.

         P6.4 Plan: Gate D Test (Restart/Recovery) - Revised

         Objective

         Create make test-recovery target with deterministic Gate D tests
    following the Gate C pattern.

         Gate D Criterion (Doc 3 Appendix A): "Restart + resume works mid-step
    and at awaiting_review with
         no lost artifacts"

         Approach (Revised per Co-Developer-1)

         1. New dedicated file apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py -
    follows Gate C pattern
         2. Monkeypatch LLM calls - reuse stub_llm_nodes pattern from
    test_gate_c.py
         3. Test at Step 2 awaiting_review - Run to Step 1 review → approve →
  run
    to Step 2 review → restart
         4. Verify Step 1 artifacts preserved - Cross-step artifact preservation
    is key
         5. Seed and verify messages - WorkflowState.messages should survive
    restart
         6. No mid-step recovery - Focus on awaiting_review checkpoint
    (deterministic)
         7. Simulate process death - dispose engine, create new checkpointer/
    graph instances

         Files

         | File                                      | Action | Purpose
              |
         
  |-------------------------------------------|--------|------------------
    ---------------------------
         -----|
         | apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py | Create | Deterministic
    Gate D tests with
         monkeypatching   |
         | Makefile                                  | Edit   | Add
  test-recovery
    target, update .PHONY and
         help |

         Test Scenarios (Revised)

         | ID     | Scenario                         | Test
    |

    
  |--------|----------------------------------|--------------------------------
    ------------|
         | D1-D4  | Comprehensive restart/recovery   |
    test_restart_preserves_state_and_artifacts |
         | Resume | Workflow continues after restart |
    test_resume_continues_after_restart        |

         Key Design Decisions

         1. 2 focused tests instead of 4 separate - comprehensive coverage, less
    duplication
         2. Test at Step 2 - Verifies cross-step artifact preservation (Step 1
    artifacts survive)
         3. Seed and verify messages - Gate D explicitly requires "conversation
    history preserved"
         4. SolverCheckpointSaver only - per DECISIONS.md
         5. Only run test_gate_d.py - Don't include legacy tests with LLM
    dependencies

         Mid-Step Recovery: Pragmatic Limitation

         Spec says: "Restart + resume works mid-step and at awaiting_review"

         Limitation: Mid-step recovery is not tested in test_gate_d.py due to:
         - LangGraph checkpoints at interrupt boundaries, not mid-generation
         - Simulating a crash during LLM streaming is non-deterministic
         - Would require mocking checkpoint timing or process signals

         Pragmatic approach: Focus on awaiting_review checkpoints which:
         - Are the primary human interaction points
         - Have deterministic state
         - Cover the "no lost artifacts" criterion

         This is acknowledged as a partial Gate D implementation. If full mid-
    step testing is required,
         invoke deviation protocol or extend with integration tests that use
  real
    LLM calls
         (non-deterministic).

         Implementation

         1. Test Structure

         """
         Gate D Integration Tests - Restart/Recovery

         P6.4: Tests Gate D criterion:
           "Restart + resume works mid-step and at awaiting_review with no lost
    artifacts."

         Uses monkeypatched LLM calls for deterministic, offline testing.
         Graph-level tests only (Gate E covers API endpoints).
         """

         class TestGateDRestartRecovery:
             """Gate D: State survives restart; artifacts and messages
    preserved."""

             @pytest.mark.asyncio
             async def test_restart_preserves_state_and_artifacts(self,
    stub_llm_nodes):
                 """D1-D4: State, artifacts, and messages survive process
    restart.

                 GIVEN a workflow at Step 2 awaiting_review with Step 1
  artifacts
    and messages
                 WHEN process restarts (engine disposed, new checkpointer/graph
    created)
                 THEN current_step == REQUIREMENTS
                 AND step_state.status == AWAITING_REVIEW
                 AND methodology["problem_definition"] exists (Step 1 artifact)
                 AND messages preserved
                 """

             @pytest.mark.asyncio
             async def test_resume_continues_after_restart(self,
  stub_llm_nodes):
                 """Resume: Workflow can continue after restart.

                 GIVEN a workflow restarted at Step 2 awaiting_review
                 WHEN I approve Step 2 after restart
                 THEN workflow advances to Step 3
                 """

         2. Test Flow (test_restart_preserves_state_and_artifacts)

         # Phase 1: Build to Step 2 awaiting_review
         engine1 = create_async_engine(settings.postgres_url, echo=False)
         factory1 = async_sessionmaker(engine1, class_=AsyncSession,
    expire_on_commit=False)
         saver1 = SolverCheckpointSaver(factory1, engine=engine1)
         graph1 = create_graph(saver1)

         # Initial state with seeded messages
         initial_state = WorkflowState(
             ...,
             messages=[Message(role="user", content="Initial problem
    description")],
         )

         # Run to Step 1 review
         result1 = await graph1.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
         assert_interrupted(result1)
         assert result1["current_step"] == StepName.PROBLEM_DEFINITION

         # Approve Step 1 → advance to Step 2 review
         await graph1.aupdate_state(config, {"human_decision":
    HumanAction.APPROVE}, as_node="review")
         result2 = await graph1.ainvoke(None, config)
         assert_interrupted(result2)
         assert result2["current_step"] == StepName.REQUIREMENTS
         assert "problem_definition" in result2.get("methodology", {})

         # Dispose to simulate process death
         await engine1.dispose()

         # Phase 2: Create completely NEW everything, same thread_id
         engine2 = create_async_engine(settings.postgres_url, echo=False)
         factory2 = async_sessionmaker(engine2, class_=AsyncSession,
    expire_on_commit=False)
         saver2 = SolverCheckpointSaver(factory2, engine=engine2)
         graph2 = create_graph(saver2)

         # Verify state persisted
         snapshot = await graph2.aget_state(config)
         assert snapshot is not None, "State should persist after restart"
         assert snapshot.values["current_step"] == StepName.REQUIREMENTS.value
         assert snapshot.values["step_state"]["status"] ==
    StepStatus.AWAITING_REVIEW.value
         assert "problem_definition" in snapshot.values.get("methodology", {})
         # Messages verification (if in checkpoint state)

         3. Makefile Target

         test-recovery:
             @echo "Running Gate D tests (restart/recovery)..."
             PYTHONPATH=apps/api .venv/bin/pytest apps/api/tests/integration/
    test_gate_d.py -v
             @echo "✓ Gate D tests passed"

         Implementation Steps

         1. Create apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py:
           - Copy fixture pattern from test_gate_c.py (stub_llm_nodes,
    checkpointer)
           - Implement 2 focused test scenarios
           - Use restart simulation pattern (engine dispose/recreate)
           - Test at Step 2 with Step 1 artifacts
         2. Update Makefile:
           - Add .PHONY: test-recovery
           - Add help text: make test-recovery   - Run Gate D tests (restart/
    recovery)
           - Add test-recovery target after test-gates
         3. Run make test-recovery to verify

         Verification

         make test-recovery

         # Expected:
         # tests/integration/
    
  test_gate_d.py::TestGateDRestartRecovery::test_restart_preserves_state_and_ar
    ti
         facts PASSED
         # tests/integration/
    
  test_gate_d.py::TestGateDRestartRecovery::test_resume_continues_after_restart
         PASSED
         # ✓ Gate D tests passed

         Negative Patterns to Avoid

         1. No real LLM calls - tests must be deterministic and offline
         2. Use SolverCheckpointSaver - not PostgresSaver (per DECISIONS.md)
         3. Don't mix Gate C/E concerns - test-recovery is Gate D only
         4. No API endpoints - graph-level only (Gate E covers API)
         5. Don't include legacy tests - Only run test_gate_d.py, not
    test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py
         6. No mid-step recovery - Focus on awaiting_review checkpoint points

         Exit Criteria

         - make test-recovery exits with code 0
         - Tests verify: state survives, artifacts preserved, messages
  preserved,
    resume works
         - Tests run without LLM/network access
         - Tests prove restart doesn't lose state or artifacts

         Dependencies

         - PostgreSQL running (make dev-db)
         - No LLM API keys required (monkeypatched)
    </information only> no action required

     "End-to-End" implies).
   * Decision: The Senior Developer plan for P6.5 needs to clarify if E2E means
     "full stack including LLM" or "full stack with mocked LLM". Given the
     previous directives, "mocked LLM" is preferred for CI/CD stability, but
     true E2E often implies real external systems.
   * Refinement: Doc 0 says "no UI required", implying API-level E2E. Doc 3 Gate
     E says "Full interactive flow".

   async def test_sse_stream_connects(self, client):

  response:

> I partially disagree with the plan as written. It’s on the right path
    (HTTP‑level tests, ASGITransport, no external server/LLM), but it doesn’t
    actually satisfy Gate E as specified.

    Key gaps vs directives/spec:

    - Gate E requires a full SSE event sequence (workflow.started → step.started
  →
      artifact.delta → artifact.final → step.awaiting_review, then step.approved
  →
      next step) per docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md. “Test what exists and
      document gaps” is not acceptable for an acceptance gate; it needs
      implementation or a deviation entry in docs/DECISIONS.md.
    - The current SSE endpoint only yields a snapshot + heartbeat (apps/api/
      routes/workflows.py); the plan doesn’t add real event emission, so tests
      won’t be able to assert the required event order.
    - The plan reuses the Gate C stub, which returns Pass 2 outputs. POST /
      workflows starts Pass 1 (definition), so a Pass‑2‑style stub will likely
      mis‑align with Pass‑1 validation/flow and can cause loops or invalid
  state.
    - Spec/README require make e2e, but Makefile only has test-e2e. Gate E
  should
      add an e2e target or alias in Makefile to match the spec.

    Best path to meet the objective:

    - Implement SSE event emission, not just tests. Add a small in‑memory broker
      (queue + backlog per workflow) and publish events from the API layer after
      graph runs. Keep it minimal (no new architecture beyond a tiny helper in
      apps/api/routes/workflows.py or a small module under
  apps/api/application/).
    - Update /workflows/{id}/stream to replay backlog then stream live events
  with
      keep‑alive comments. This allows the Gate E flow to work even if the
  stream
      connects after workflow creation.
    - Make the LLM stub pass‑aware: return methodology payloads for Pass 1 and
      package payloads for Pass 2; override validate_output to always pass in
      tests so the flow is deterministic.
    - Add an e2e Makefile target (or alias to test-e2e) to satisfy the command
  in
      docs/spec/0_SOLVER-Development-Directive.md and README.md.

    Negative patterns to avoid:

    - Treating missing SSE events as “acceptable gaps” instead of implementing
  or
      logging a deviation.
    - Using a Pass‑2 stub for Pass‑1 execution in API tests.
    - Relying on TestClient for SSE without streamed line parsing + explicit
  stop
      conditions (risk of hanging).
    - Expanding Gate E to cover revise/message beyond what the spec requires if
  it
      adds flakiness.
      
      
      Below is a concrete, minimal event‑broker sketch plus a tighter Gate E
  test
    matrix that fits the current apps/api/routes/workflows.py flow and the
    LangGraph execution model.

    Event‑Broker Sketch (Minimal, In‑Process, Backlog + Live)
    Files (suggested):

    - apps/api/application/event_stream.py (new): broker + helpers
    - apps/api/routes/workflows.py (wire publish + stream)

    Sketch (pseudo‑code, minimal surface):

    # apps/api/application/event_stream.py
    from __future__ import annotations
    import asyncio
    from collections import deque
    from dataclasses import dataclass
    from typing import AsyncIterator, Deque, Dict, Optional

    @dataclass
    class SSEEvent:
        event_type: str
        payload: dict

    class EventBroker:
        def __init__(self, backlog_size: int = 200):
            self._queues: Dict[str, asyncio.Queue[SSEEvent]] = {}
            self._backlog: Dict[str, Deque[SSEEvent]] = {}
            self._lock = asyncio.Lock()
            self._backlog_size = backlog_size

        async def publish(self, workflow_id: str, event: SSEEvent) -> None:
            async with self._lock:
                backlog = self._backlog.setdefault(
                    workflow_id, deque(maxlen=self._backlog_size)
                )
                backlog.append(event)
                queue = self._queues.get(workflow_id)
                if queue:
                    queue.put_nowait(event)

        async def subscribe(self, workflow_id: str) -> AsyncIterator[SSEEvent]:
            queue = asyncio.Queue()
            async with self._lock:
                self._queues[workflow_id] = queue
                backlog = list(self._backlog.get(workflow_id, []))
            for event in backlog:
                yield event
            try:
                while True:
                    event = await queue.get()
                    yield event
            finally:
                async with self._lock:
                    if self._queues.get(workflow_id) is queue:
                        del self._queues[workflow_id]

    _broker: Optional[EventBroker] = None

    def get_event_broker() -> EventBroker:
        global _broker
        if _broker is None:
            _broker = EventBroker()
        return _broker

    Publishing Hooks (in apps/api/routes/workflows.py)
    Add small helpers next to existing SSE helpers:

    - build_sse_payload(workflow, step_execution, event_type, data=None,
      artifact_id=None)
        - Use the Doc 3 payload shape: event_id, event_type, workflow_id,
          instance_id, pass_type, step_number, step_name, timestamp,
  artifact_id,
          data.
        - Use workflow.current_pass / workflow.current_step to fill pass_type/
          step_name/step_number when step_execution is None.
    - emit_event(workflow, step_execution, event_type, data=None,
      artifact_id=None)
        - await broker.publish(workflow.workflow_id, SSEEvent(event_type,
          payload))

    Publish points (minimal coverage to satisfy Gate E):

    - POST /workflows:
        - before graph.ainvoke: workflow.started, step.started (step 1, pass 1).
        - after graph.ainvoke + sync_db_from_state:
            - emit artifact.delta events by chunking result["step_state"]
              ["output"] (e.g., JSON string split into 2–4 chunks).
            - emit artifact.final.
            - emit step.awaiting_review with data containing validation summary
              and output.
    - POST /workflows/{id}/actions/approve:
        - immediately after DB update: step.approved.
        - after graph.ainvoke: step.started for Step 2, then
  artifact.delta(>=1),
          artifact.final, step.awaiting_review.
    - POST /workflows/{id}/actions/revise (optional for Gate E):
      step.revision_requested then normal step events after re‑run.

    Stream endpoint update:

    - In stream_workflow, replace the static snapshot/heartbeat loop with broker
      subscription:
        - If no backlog, you may optionally send a one‑time snapshot (current
          state) before live events.
        - Prefer backlog first so Gate E ordering is deterministic.

    Why this matches current graph behavior:
    POST /workflows triggers graph to run Step 1 → review interrupt. That’s the
    perfect point to emit artifact.final + step.awaiting_review. approve resumes
    the graph and should land at Step 2 review; emitting events after the resume
    mirrors the real flow without touching the graph internals.

    ———

    Tighter Gate E Test Matrix (Deterministic, SSE + REST)
    Test location: apps/api/tests/e2e/test_gate_e.py (new)
    Transport: httpx.AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app=main.app))
    Monkeypatch: pass‑aware stub for orchestration.nodes.generate_step_output +
    validate_output so Pass 1 returns methodology structure and always
  validates.

    Test helper: collect_sse_events(stream, stop_predicate, timeout=5s) parses
  SSE
    frames (event: + data:) into (event_type, payload) list.

    Matrix (minimal + spec‑aligned):

    1. E1/E3: create + SSE event order to first review

    - Action: POST /api/v1/workflows then GET /api/v1/workflows/{id}/stream
    - Stop when: first step.awaiting_review
    - Assert order:
        - workflow.started
        - step.started (step_number=1, step_name="problem_definition",
          pass_type="definition")
        - artifact.delta (>=1)
        - artifact.final
        - step.awaiting_review
    - Assert payload fields: event_id, workflow_id matches, timestamp present;
      data.delta present for delta events.

    2. E4: approve → Step 2 starts via SSE

    - Action: open stream, then POST /actions/approve
    - Stop when: step.awaiting_review for step 2
    - Assert order after approval:
        - step.approved (step 1)
        - step.started (step 2)
        - artifact.delta (>=1)
        - artifact.final
        - step.awaiting_review (step 2)
    - Assert: step_number=2, step_name="requirements",
      pass_type="definition" (still Pass 1).

    3. E2 (basic REST sanity, folded into E1)

    - Action: GET /api/v1/workflows/{id} after create
    - Assert: response workflow_id matches, current_step is problem_definition,
      step_state.status is awaiting_review.

    Notes:

    - Avoid revise/message tests here (Gate C scope).
    - Make artifact.delta assertion “>=1” to tolerate chunking strategy.
    - Always break SSE loops once the expected final event is seen to avoid
  hangs.
    
    
    Below is a refined mapping that aligns event payloads with STATUS_TO_EVENT
  in
    apps/api/routes/workflows.py and keeps tests consistent with the current SSE
    format.

    Mapping Rules (Align With STATUS_TO_EVENT)

    - For any step-state event, set event_type to STATUS_TO_EVENT[status] and
      include data.status and data.phase.
    - event: line and payload["event_type"] must match exactly.
    - Only workflow.started and workflow.completed bypass STATUS_TO_EVENT (do
  not
      infer from status).
    - artifact.delta and artifact.final do not need status mapping; include only
      delta or artifact_id in data.

    Status to Event Mapping (Use As Source of Truth)

    - not_started -> step.started (phase: received)
    - in_progress -> step.started (phase: analyzing/structuring/validating)
    - awaiting_clarification -> step.awaiting_clarification (phase: eliciting)
    - awaiting_review -> step.awaiting_review (phase: reviewing)
    - approved -> step.approved (phase: complete)
    - revision_requested -> step.revision_requested (phase: structuring)

    Payload Shape (Matches Existing Stream Snapshot)

    def build_sse_payload(
        workflow,
        step_execution,
        event_type=None,
        data=None,
        artifact_id=None,
    ):
        status = step_execution.status.value if step_execution else None
        phase = step_execution.phase.value if step_execution else None

        resolved_event = event_type or STATUS_TO_EVENT.get(status,
  "step.started")

        payload = {
            "event_id": str(uuid4()),
            "event_type": resolved_event,
            "workflow_id": workflow.workflow_id,
            "instance_id": str(workflow.instance_id),
            "pass_type": workflow.current_pass.value,
            "step_number": workflow.current_step_number,
            "step_name": workflow.current_step.value,
            "timestamp": datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
            "artifact_id": artifact_id,
            "data": {},
        }

        if status or phase:
            payload["data"].update(
                {"status": status, "phase": phase}
            )
        if data:
            payload["data"].update(data)

        return resolved_event, payload

    Event-Specific Data (Keep Minimal and Spec‑Aligned)

    - step.awaiting_review: include data.output and data.validation (per Doc 3).
    - step.awaiting_clarification: include data.questions.
    - artifact.delta: include data.delta only.
    - artifact.final: include artifact_id and optional data summary.

    Test Alignment Rules (E2E Assertions)

    - For any step.* event:
        - assert payload["event_type"] == event_name
        - assert STATUS_TO_EVENT[payload["data"]["status"]] == event_name
        - assert payload["data"]["phase"] is present
    - For workflow.started/workflow.completed: check event_name and core fields
      only.
    - For artifact.delta/artifact.final: check event_name and data fields, skip
      status mapping.

    This keeps the broker-published events and stream snapshot behavior
  consistent
    with apps/api/routes/workflows.py while making the Gate E tests
  deterministic
    and aligned to the spec.

    Where I agree:

    - Gate E requires the full SSE sequence; “test what exists” is not

      without a deviation. Implementation is required.
    - Current SSE is snapshot‑only; tests cannot pass without event emission.
    - Pass‑aware stubs are required because POST /workflows runs Pass 1.
    - make e2e must exist per docs/spec/0_SOLVER-Development-Directive.md and
      README.md.

    Refinements I recommend:

    - Event mapping must use STATUS_TO_EVENT for step‑state events. Event
  emission
      should set event: and payload event_type to the mapped value, with
      data.status and data.phase included. This avoids mismatches between stream
      events and tests.
    - Backlog replay is necessary. Without backlog, a stream opened after
  workflow
      creation will miss workflow.started and step.started. A small in‑memory
      broker with backlog per workflow is minimal and acceptable for MVP (single
      worker).
    - Emit events at API boundaries (after graph.ainvoke and DB sync) instead of
      inside graph nodes. This keeps architecture clean and respects the
  layering
      rules.
    - Keep tests focused on the positive flow (create → approve → step 2) and
      assert only “>=1 artifact.delta” to tolerate synthetic chunking.

    Potential risks to avoid:

    - Emitting step.* events without aligning to STATUS_TO_EVENT (will fail the
      Gate E assertions).
    - Using Pass‑2 stubs for Pass‑1 execution (will cause validation/flow
      mismatches).
    - Relying on test-e2e only; Gate E spec calls for make e2e.

    Net: The plan is on the right track once it includes the event broker + SSE
    emission and the Pass‑aware stub. With those adjustments, it should satisfy
    Gate E without deviations.

         Implementation

         1. EventBroker (apps/api/application/event_stream.py)

         """Minimal SSE Event Broker for Gate E compliance."""
         from __future__ import annotations
         import asyncio
         from collections import deque
         from dataclasses import dataclass
         from typing import AsyncIterator, Deque, Dict, Optional

         @dataclass
         class SSEEvent:
             event_type: str
             payload: dict

         class EventBroker:
             """In-memory event broker with backlog + live streaming."""

             def __init__(self, backlog_size: int = 200):
                 self._queues: Dict[str, asyncio.Queue[SSEEvent]] = {}
                 self._backlog: Dict[str, Deque[SSEEvent]] = {}
                 self._lock = asyncio.Lock()
                 self._backlog_size = backlog_size

             async def publish(self, workflow_id: str, event: SSEEvent) -> None:
                 """Publish event to backlog and any active subscriber."""
                 async with self._lock:
                     backlog = self._backlog.setdefault(
                         workflow_id, deque(maxlen=self._backlog_size)
                     )
                     backlog.append(event)
                     queue = self._queues.get(workflow_id)
                     if queue:
                         queue.put_nowait(event)

             async def subscribe(self, workflow_id: str) ->
    AsyncIterator[SSEEvent]:
                 """Subscribe to events. Yields backlog first, then live
    events."""
                 queue: asyncio.Queue[SSEEvent] = asyncio.Queue()
                 async with self._lock:
                     self._queues[workflow_id] = queue
                     backlog = list(self._backlog.get(workflow_id, []))
                 # Replay backlog
                 for event in backlog:
                     yield event
                 # Stream live events
                 try:
                     while True:
                         event = await queue.get()
                         yield event
                 finally:
                     async with self._lock:
                         if self._queues.get(workflow_id) is queue:
                             del self._queues[workflow_id]

         # Module singleton
         _broker: Optional[EventBroker] = None

         def get_event_broker() -> EventBroker:
             global _broker
             if _broker is None:
                 _broker = EventBroker()
             return _broker

         2. Event Emission Helpers (add to routes/workflows.py)

         from application.event_stream import get_event_broker, SSEEvent

         def build_sse_payload(
             workflow,
             step_execution,
             event_type: str = None,
             data: dict = None,
             artifact_id: str = None,
         ) -> tuple[str, dict]:
             """Build SSE payload aligned with STATUS_TO_EVENT mapping."""
             status = step_execution.status.value if step_execution else None
             phase = step_execution.phase.value if step_execution else None

             # Use STATUS_TO_EVENT for step-state events
             resolved_event = event_type or STATUS_TO_EVENT.get(status,
    "step.started")

             payload = {
                 "event_id": str(uuid4()),
                 "event_type": resolved_event,
                 "workflow_id": workflow.workflow_id,
                 "instance_id": str(workflow.instance_id),
                 "pass_type": workflow.current_pass.value,
                 "step_number": workflow.current_step_number,
                 "step_name": workflow.current_step.value,
                 "timestamp": datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
                 "artifact_id": artifact_id,
                 "data": {"status": status, "phase": phase} if status else {},
             }
             if data:
                 payload["data"].update(data)
             return resolved_event, payload

         async def emit_event(workflow, step_execution, event_type=None,
    data=None, artifact_id=None):
             """Emit SSE event to broker."""
             broker = get_event_broker()
             resolved_type, payload = build_sse_payload(workflow,
  step_execution,
    event_type, data,
         artifact_id)
             await broker.publish(workflow.workflow_id, SSEEvent(resolved_type,
    payload))

         3. Publish Points (in routes/workflows.py)

         POST /workflows (create_workflow):
         # Before graph.ainvoke:
         await emit_event(workflow, None, "workflow.started")
         await emit_event(workflow, step_execution, "step.started")

         # After graph.ainvoke + sync_db_from_state:
         # Synthetic artifact.delta (chunk output into 2-3 pieces)
         output_str = json.dumps(result.get("step_state", {}).get("output", {}))
         for i, chunk in enumerate(chunk_string(output_str, 3)):
             await emit_event(workflow, step_execution, "artifact.delta",
    {"delta": chunk, "index": i})
         await emit_event(workflow, step_execution, "artifact.final",
    artifact_id=str(artifact.id))
         await emit_event(workflow, step_execution)  # Uses STATUS_TO_EVENT →
    step.awaiting_review

         POST /actions/approve:
         # After DB update:
         await emit_event(workflow, step_execution, "step.approved")

         # After graph.ainvoke for next step:
         await emit_event(workflow, new_step, "step.started")
         # ... artifact.delta, artifact.final, step.awaiting_review

         4. Update /stream Endpoint

         @router.get("/{workflow_id}/stream")
         async def stream_workflow(workflow_id: str, ...):
             broker = get_event_broker()

             async def event_generator():
                 async for event in broker.subscribe(workflow_id):
                     yield format_sse_event(event.event_type, event.payload)
                     # Heartbeat between events
                     yield format_sse_comment("keep-alive")

             return StreamingResponse(
                 event_generator(),
                 media_type="text/event-stream",
                 headers={"Cache-Control": "no-cache", "X-Accel-Buffering":
    "no"},
             )

         5. Pass-Aware Stub (tests/e2e/conftest.py)

         @pytest.fixture
         def stub_llm_nodes(monkeypatch):
             """Pass-aware stub: methodology for Pass 1, packages for Pass 2."""

             async def fake_generate_step_output(state: WorkflowState) -> dict:
                 step = state.current_step
                 pass_type = state.current_pass

                 if pass_type == PassType.DEFINITION:
                     # Pass 1: Return methodology doc structure
                     return {
                         "document_type": "methodology",
                         "version": "v1",
                         "content": f"Methodology for {step.value}",
                         "sections": ["overview", "approach", "deliverables"],
                     }
                 else:
                     # Pass 2: Return step package (reuse Gate C stub logic)
                     if step == StepName.PROBLEM_DEFINITION:
                         return { ... }  # ProblemDefinitionPackage structure
                     # ... etc

             async def fake_validate_output(step, output, pass_type=None):
                 return ValidationResult(passed=True, errors=[], warnings=[])

             monkeypatch.setattr("orchestration.nodes.generate_step_output",
    fake_generate_step_output)
             monkeypatch.setattr("orchestration.nodes.validate_output",
    fake_validate_output)

         6. Test Structure (tests/e2e/test_gate_e.py)

         """Gate E Integration Tests - API + SSE Event Sequence."""

         API_PREFIX = "/api/v1/workflows"

         async def collect_sse_events(response, stop_on: str, timeout: float =
    5.0) -> list:
             """Parse SSE frames until stop_on event_type or timeout."""
             events = []
             async for line in response.aiter_lines():
                 if line.startswith("data:"):
                     payload = json.loads(line[5:].strip())
                     events.append((payload["event_type"], payload))
                     if payload["event_type"] == stop_on:
                         break
             return events

         class TestGateESSEFlow:
             """Gate E: Full SSE event sequence verification."""

             @pytest.mark.asyncio
             async def test_create_workflow_sse_sequence(self, client):
                 """E1/E3: Create workflow and verify SSE event order to first
    review."""
                 # Create workflow
                 resp = await client.post(API_PREFIX, json={
                     "problem": "Gate E test problem",
                     "created_by": "test-actor"
                 })
                 assert resp.status_code == 201
                 workflow_id = resp.json()["workflow_id"]

                 # Connect to stream and collect events
                 async with client.stream("GET", f"{API_PREFIX}/{workflow_id}/
    stream") as stream:
                     events = await collect_sse_events(stream,
    "step.awaiting_review")

                 # Assert event order
                 event_types = [e[0] for e in events]
                 assert event_types[0] == "workflow.started"
                 assert event_types[1] == "step.started"
                 assert event_types.count("artifact.delta") >= 1
                 assert "artifact.final" in event_types
                 assert event_types[-1] == "step.awaiting_review"

                 # Assert payload fields
                 for event_type, payload in events:
                     assert payload["workflow_id"] == workflow_id
                     assert "timestamp" in payload
                     if event_type.startswith("step."):
                         assert payload["data"]["status"] is not None

             @pytest.mark.asyncio
             async def test_approve_advances_via_sse(self, client):
                 """E4: Approve Step 1 → Step 2 starts, verify via SSE."""
                 # Create workflow (reaches step.awaiting_review for Step 1)
                 resp = await client.post(API_PREFIX, json={
                     "problem": "Gate E approve test",
                     "created_by": "test-actor"
                 })
                 workflow_id = resp.json()["workflow_id"]

                 # Open stream, then approve
                 async with client.stream("GET", f"{API_PREFIX}/{workflow_id}/
    stream") as stream:
                     # Skip to awaiting_review
                     await collect_sse_events(stream, "step.awaiting_review")

                     # Approve Step 1
                     approve_resp = await client.post(
                         f"{API_PREFIX}/{workflow_id}/actions/approve",
                         json={"actor_id": "test-actor"}
                     )
                     assert approve_resp.status_code == 200

                     # Collect events for Step 2
                     events = await collect_sse_events(stream,
    "step.awaiting_review")

                 # Assert Step 2 sequence
                 event_types = [e[0] for e in events]
                 assert "step.approved" in event_types
                 assert "step.started" in event_types

                 # Verify Step 2 metadata
                 step_started = next(p for t, p in events if t ==
  "step.started")
                 assert step_started["step_number"] == 2
                 assert step_started["step_name"] == "requirements"

         Verification

         # Expected:
         # tests/e2e/
    test_gate_e.py::TestGateESSEFlow::test_create_workflow_sse_sequence PASSED
         # tests/e2e/

         # ✓ Gate E tests passed

         Negative Patterns to Avoid

         1. No real LLM calls - deterministic via pass-aware monkeypatch
         2. Don't emit inside graph nodes - emit at API boundaries only
         3. Don't skip backlog - late-connecting streams need event history
         4. Don't mismatch event_type - always use STATUS_TO_EVENT for
  step-state
    events
         5. Don't use Pass 2 stubs for Pass 1 - methodology output for
  DEFINITION
    pass
         6. Don't hang on SSE - always have stop condition + timeout in tests

         Exit Criteria

         - make e2e exits with code 0
         - SSE event sequence verified: workflow.started → step.started →
    artifact.delta(>=1) →
         artifact.final → step.awaiting_review
         - Approve action triggers: step.approved → step.started (Step 2) → ...
  →
    step.awaiting_review
         - All events use STATUS_TO_EVENT mapping with data.status/phase
         - Tests deterministic (no LLM/network)
    </information only>
